# Open research: reproducible papers with code and benchmarks

Open research: white papers, reproducible code and benchmarks, one directory per project (MIT licensed, see [`LICENSE`](LICENSE)). Topics run from verification markets, scoring rules and decentralised training to LLM chip-design loops, task-specific AI accelerators, robot manufacturing, digital twins, engineered clothing and open-source housing.
Moved here from the former `oss-research-monorepo` repository with its history. The homepage is built from [`_homepage/`](_homepage) and published at [`/research/`](https://duncan-haywood.github.io/monorepo-projects/research/) on the repository's GitHub Pages site.

## All projects

- [`research/decentralized-verification-markets`](decentralized-verification-markets) — peer-prediction and prediction-market mechanisms for verifying decentralized ML training, with adversarial evaluation.
- [`research/market-routing`](market-routing) — cost-function markets as expert routers: LMSR = Hedge, quadratic potential = sparsemax; regret/loss bounds verified, continual-learning simulation.
- [`research/wagering-modular-experts`](wagering-modular-experts) — self-financed wagering mechanisms as verifiable routers for modular, continually learning experts; shows plain WSWM locks in under recurring regimes and a fixed-share tax fixes it at the cost of subsidising free-riders.
- [`research/property-elicitation-verification`](property-elicitation-verification) — property elicitation (quantile/expectile/Bregman scores, non-elicitability of variance) applied to pricing tail-drift tolerance thresholds in verifiable ML training.
- [`research/verification-game`](verification-game) — inspection-game economics of refereed verification: stake substitutes for re-execution, extra verifiers free-ride, and optimal tolerance/stake under floating-point drift.
- [`research/verifier-bribery`](verifier-bribery) — bribing verifiers in refereed verification: closed-form bribe floor, a sharp collusion-proof stake that falls as 1/m, and jackpots that raise the floor one-for-one.
- [`research/reproducible-refereed-training`](reproducible-refereed-training) — measured float32 reduction-order drift, RepOps-style canonical order, Merkle-committed training traces with a teacher-forced referee; per-step tolerance is a cumulative hidden-deviation budget.
- [`research/private-markets`](private-markets) — differentially private LMSR via the binary-tree mechanism: path-wise loss bound, and the accuracy/subsidy cost of privacy.
- [`research/audit-dynamics`](audit-dynamics) — no-regret learning in the refereed-verification inspection game: conserved potential, closed-form cycle period, averages converge but per-round audit probability collapses; optimistic Hedge converges.
- [`research/multi-verifier-audit`](multi-verifier-audit) — m verifiers in the inspection game: detection pinned at s/(s+S), cheating rises with m under split rewards, symmetric equilibrium is a saddle (volunteer's dilemma), bandit learners need an exploration floor.
- [`research/holdout-market`](holdout-market) — paying for model improvements against a hidden holdout: telescoping budget, a payment-feedback overfitting attack (paid 0.15 while true loss worsens 0.37), and the Ladder fix with its measured cost.
- [`research/spot-check-slashing`](spot-check-slashing) — sampled audits of committed training traces: payoff is convex in corrupted steps, deterrence iff `min(F, f·k) ≥ T·g`, so stake and audits are complements and stake must scale with job length.
- [`research/quantized-reports`](quantized-reports) — finite-precision probability reports under proper scoring: optimal-grid regret law matched to 0.3%, uniform grids lose a `ln N` factor under log score, raw minifloat formats are ~13× worse than symmetrised.
- [`research/effort-elicitation`](effort-elicitation) — paying verifiers for costly checking effort with proper scoring rules: implementable efforts = concave envelope of the value curve, exact Brier rent `(n−1)/(a+b)`, log score needs half Brier's payout spread on rare-fault priors.
- [`research/expert-pooling`](expert-pooling) — pooling calibrated experts with shared training data: Bayes-optimal extremization `a* = n/(1+(n−1)ρ)` verified numerically, saturation at `n_eff → 1/ρ`, asymmetric robustness, and online learning of the exponent.
- [`research/effort-contracts`](effort-contracts) — scoring rules as limited-liability effort contracts: shirking threshold `2c/(kκ²)`, first best needs a fee, otherwise the principal keeps 48–68% of surplus.
- [`research/peer-prediction-effort`](peer-prediction-effort) — peer prediction without ground truth: output agreement pays 1.0 for laziness vs 0.68 for honesty, Dasgupta–Ghosh payoff `2p(1-p)g_i g_j` exact, effort as a coordination game with threshold `c/(2p(1-p)κ²)`, and closed-form gold-check rate `√(c·g_H/G)`.
- [`research/decision-regret-transfer`](decision-regret-transfer) — from a verifier's excess proper score to a certified cap on accept/audit/slash decision regret: exact calibration functions (Brier ε², log KL), linear transfer only for non-proper hinge, audit action tightens the guarantee.
- [`research/reject-surrogates`](reject-surrogates) — polyhedral surrogates for accept/reject/escalate verifiers: minimiser embeds the Bayes action, linear regret transfer with constant exactly `2d`, logistic transfer ratio `2d(1-d)/δ`, and a negative SGD result.
- [`research/sybil-stake`](sybil-stake) — identity splitting vs merging under stake-weighted pools: split-invariant iff the weight is linear in stake, per-head optimum `√(Rn/c)−n`, sharp deterrence fee `Rn/((n+1)(n+2))`, wagering split-neutral.
- [`research/robust-aggregation`](robust-aggregation) — Byzantine-robust aggregation of verifier reports: mean bias `βB` unbounded, median bias `σΦ⁻¹(1/(2(1−β)))` independent of B, trimmed-mean closed form for τ≥β verified by simulation.
- [`research/sequential-slashing`](sequential-slashing) — anytime-valid slashing of miscalibrated verifiers: peeking z-test falsely slashes 37% of honest verifiers (nominal 5%), an e-process holds ≤α by Ville, detection delay ≈ `ln(1/α)/KL` (within 12%), q-free mixture costs ×1.36.
- [`research/gossip-consensus`](gossip-consensus) — pairwise gossip for decentralised training: exact contraction `(n−2)/(2(n−1))≈½` per matching round (~20 rounds at any n), noise floor `(n−1)(n−2)s²/n`, one stubborn node captures consensus at rate `1−1/(n(n−1))`, trust-region clipping caps drift at `τ/(n(n−1))` per step.
- [`research/verifier-complements`](verifier-complements) — verifiers as informational complements: decision value is zero below a closed-form committee size while mutual information is submodular; myopic hiring buys nothing and leave-one-out pay is a sawtooth that vanishes.
- [`research/dispute-arity`](dispute-arity) — k-ary bisection dispute games: optimal arity solves `k ln k − k + 1 = a/b` (binary only if `a/b ≤ 0.386`), 1.9–6× cheaper than binary at T=10⁶, adaptive schedules gain ≤1%, leaf-size trade-off, latency as extra round cost.
- [`research/compute-procurement`](compute-procurement) — buying k units of decentralised compute by reverse auction: exact VCG payment `k(k+1)/(n+1)` is exactly 2× winners' cost, optimal reserve is exactly `v/2`, first-price equilibrium matches by revenue equivalence.
- [`research/delayed-routing`](delayed-routing) — Hedge/LMSR routing with delayed feedback: regret `≤ ln N/η + ηT/8 + ηD/2` (mean delay inflates horizon by `1+4d̄`), 12× regret from ignoring delay against a reactive adversary, delay-tuned rate too cautious on oblivious data, LMSR subsidy ~20× at mean delay 5.
- [`research/replicated-execution`](replicated-execution) — random k-fold replication with slashing against a colluding fraction β: secret assignment makes cheating a coordination game with unique-honesty stake `Gβ^{k−1}/(1−β^{k−1})`, public assignment leaves `β^k` corruption at any stake, and assignment leaks leave a `λβ^k` floor.
- [`research/market-manipulation`](market-manipulation) — manipulating an LMSR verification market: manipulator loss `b·KL(q‖τ)` equals the extra informed rent, and manipulation raises accuracy only in a liquidity band (thin: costs `1−2q`; deep: neutral).
- [`research/audit-allocation`](audit-allocation) — scarce audit budget across heterogeneous jobs: deterrence threshold `g/(g+S)` makes it a knapsack, greedy with a 1/3 guarantee (within 1% on 91% of instances), uniform audits need up to 1.9× the budget, heterogeneity lowers deterrence cost (Jensen), stake substitutes for audits.
- [`research/churn-checkpointing`](churn-checkpointing) — checkpoint interval for preemptible decentralised training: exact Lambert-W optimum independent of restart cost, Young/Daly overshoot 5–25%, flat within 2×, synchronous efficiency wall (50% at n≈691), flaky workers cut throughput 5×.
- [`research/data-valuation-replication`](data-valuation-replication) — Shapley and leave-one-out payments under replication: Shapley pays every extra copy even when worthless, saturating at stand-alone value (`(n+2)/2`× honest), naive evaluators hand over the pot, LOO pays copies 0 but distributes 1/(n+1), dedup underpays lookalikes by 45%.
- [`research/escalation-bonds`](escalation-bonds) — escalating-bond dispute games: exact deterrence opening bond `εV/((1−ε)C−εD)` shrinks geometrically in rounds while the attacker's total outlay barely moves, and a sharp capital race where the defender needs `γ·max{C(r) ≤ B_C}` ≤ `γ·B_C`.
- [`research/local-sgd-bias`](local-sgd-bias) — local-SGD/DiLoCo-style averaging on heterogeneous quadratics: exact fixed point with saturating weights `1-(1-ηa_i)^H`, non-monotone bias to the unweighted mean, exact `a_i/w_i` correction, step-count inflation moves the fixed point 0.50→0.91, wall-clock-optimal sync interval.
- [`research/inference-substitution`](inference-substitution) — detecting a provider that serves a cheaper model on a fraction of queries: mixture e-process (valid under peeking), delay `∝ 1/(f p² χ²)`, so the cheater's best response is to leak rarely and steal `∝ √N` not `N`; closed-form audit rate and cost-optimal check rate `(hK/2Nc)^{2/3}`.
- [`research/contest-precision`](contest-precision) — winner-take-all training contests scored on a noisy holdout: effort `V a_n √m/s` with `a_2 = a_3 = 1/(2√π)` exactly, a precision ceiling `V/σ² ≤ λ_max(n)` beyond which no pure equilibrium exists (below the zero-rent limit), and the resulting optimal holdout size.
- [`research/risk-averse-scoring`](risk-averse-scoring) — risk-averse verifiers under proper scoring: log score reports exactly `odds^(1/(1+αb))`, Brier reports solve `logit p = logit r + k(2r−1)` and can be debiased exactly, a mean-`k` aggregation correction is worse than none, and a binarised lottery rule is truthful for any utility at a 2.6–27% certainty-equivalent cost.
- [`research/fiat-shamir-grinding`](fiat-shamir-grinding) — hash-derived audit challenges under free retries: cheat profit exactly `G − c/p`, deterrence needs `ln(G/c)·T/k` opened steps vs `ln2·T/k` for a beacon (10×–60×), stake is irrelevant, and the proof-of-work optimum is `x* = v/λ_p − c0`.
- [`research/anytime-liquidity`](anytime-liquidity) — LMSR with time-varying liquidity as anytime Hedge: `b_t = c*√t` costs exactly `√2` over the horizon-aware bound, doubling is 1.70× worse, and self-tuning liquidity makes the subsidy equal the accumulated mixability gap (1.3 vs 102 on easy data).
- [`research/balanced-routing`](balanced-routing) — capacity-constrained expert routing as a congestion-priced market: softmax with dual prices, verifier-computable duality-gap certificate from prices alone, sign-update balancing has a `≈2.5/√B` sampling floor plus a step-size limit cycle, closed-form two-expert price of balance, Hoeffding load audit.
- [`research/challenge-window`](challenge-window) — optimistic-verification challenge windows with bribable proposers: exact gamble-then-bribe attacker recursion, closed-form window, stake cannot shorten it under an exogenous bribe price (63 blocks for S=10…1600), bounty-funded priority fees give `S·w ≈ G/((1−ρ)θ)`.
- [`research/abstention-scoring`](abstention-scoring) — selective reporting under proper scoring: abstention iff `|p−α| < √c` (Brier), silence is evidence unless the anchor is a symmetric prior, ignoring it costs 30× the log-loss at n=12, and a stale anchor makes silence point at the wrong state.
- [`research/tail-risk-elicitation`](tail-risk-elicitation) — jointly eliciting VaR and expected shortfall of benign drift with a scale-free Rockafellar–Uryasev score: ES alone is not elicitable, misreport cost is exactly `ln r + 1/r − 1`, quantile-only tolerances leave `a/(a−1)` of the Pareto tail uncovered, and auditing tail reports breaks down at index ≤ 2.
- [`research/score-recalibration`](score-recalibration) — paying verifiers for resolution, not calibration: raw Brier/log excess is exactly `E[(r-p)²]`/`E[KL(p‖r)]`, Brier is bounded but log is not, log-score selection reverses for overconfident high-resolution verifiers (`a ≥ 2.49`), and n-sample recalibration costs `tr(HI⁻¹)/2n` (0.264/n).
- [`research/kelly-markets`](kelly-markets) — fractional-Kelly wealth markets as expert routers: risk-tolerance-weighted price, `ln(1/s)/λ` regret bound, growth-optimal `λ*=(π−p)/(q−p)`, manipulation cost `V·χ²` vs LMSR `b·KL` (7.5× stiffer at p=0.99), and a switching-truth trade-off where `λ≈0.1–0.2` beats full Kelly 3–7×.
- [`research/market-sandwich`](market-sandwich) — sandwiching an LMSR verification market: attacker profit is exactly the victim's slippage tolerance times its fair cost, needs capital `≈ εb/(1−p)`, dies to a fee `≈ v(1−p)/(2b)`; pro-rata batching keeps 99% of the extraction, random ordering cuts it 3×.
- [`research/crps-drift-scoring`](crps-drift-scoring) — CRPS vs log score for continuous drift reports: exact Gaussian closed forms, overconfidence capped at `0.234σ` under CRPS (unbounded under log), 1-Lipschitz outcome sensitivity, plug-in cost `5σ/(8√π n)`, and finite score variance for tails `ν>2` vs `ν>4`.
- [`research/pipeline-replication`](pipeline-replication) — replicate or skip? pipeline stages on unreliable workers: exact binomial drop law, replicas cannot beat the shared-domain floor `(1−ρ)^L`, a 4-stage skip budget halves workers for a 1e-3 drop rate (240 → 96), cost-optimal design shifts from skipping to replication as skips get costly.
- [`research/variance-elicitation`](variance-elicitation) — eliciting drift variance from two independent re-executions: `D=(y1−y2)²/2` unbiased with `Var=σ⁴(κ+1)/2`, exact Brier and scale-free misreport costs (`1/λ+ln λ−1`), detection needs `≈2z²(κ+1)/(λ−1)²` tasks, disjoint pairs 1.8× worse than the sample variance, heavy tails break `m^{−1/2}` concentration.
- [`research/noisy-referee`](noisy-referee) — scoring verifiers against a noisy judge: raw proper scores elicit `e0+γp` and shrink Brier incentives by `γ²`, can reverse rankings (η*=0.25), the unbiased surrogate score is exactly proper at a `1/γ` payment spread and `1/γ²` sample cost, and misestimated rates give a closed-form bias.
- [`research/focal-laziness`](focal-laziness) — peer prediction when lazy verifiers share a default answer: agreement pay is `2G·Cov`, effort is a coordination game with threshold `a*=(k/2G+s)/(pq+s)`, the all-lazy equilibrium always exists and pays as much as honesty when the default matches the prior, and gold checks needing more mass than the honest pay close it.
- [`research/audit-weighted-scoring`](audit-weighted-scoring) — scoring when truth exists only on audited tasks: report-dependent audit rates make naive scoring improper (shift `−bp(1−p)/(2(a+bp))`, full hiding for steep rates), inverse-propensity weighting is exactly proper for any audit rule, and Neyman allocation gains only 3–11% variance.
- [`research/forecast-duel`](forecast-duel) — anytime-valid duels between two verifiers: the optimal betting e-process on the Brier difference grows at exactly `KL(q‖(rA+rB)/2)`, a peeking z-test falsely declares a winner 19–38% of the time at nominal 5%, a bet mixture stays ≤3.8% and costs 15–41% more decisions.
- [`research/verifier-league`](verifier-league) — anytime-valid best-of-n verifier identification: delay set by the rival whose report midpoint sits nearest `q` (the best opposite-side rival, not the runner-up), mixture within 1.35–1.60× of oracle, elimination 4–5× cheaper, peeking declares a false best in 59% of ties vs 1.6%.
- [`research/peer-noise-rent`](peer-noise-rent) — finite-sample noise in multi-task peer prediction: the plug-in payment is 2× the sample covariance with an exact variance (matches enumeration), √2 less noisy than fresh penalty tasks, pays a constant reporter 0, and a lazy coin still earns `≈σ/√(2π)` under limited liability; deductibles and detection/ranking sample sizes in closed form.
- [`research/tangent-log`](tangent-log) — bounded-payment log score by quadratic tangent extension: strictly proper, log-identical on `[ε,1−ε]`, worst-case payment exactly `ln((1−ε)/ε)+1/(1−ε)`, 63× Brier's curvature per range at `p=10⁻³`, crossover `p(1−p)=1/(2R)`, best cutoff `ε=p`.
- [`research/shapley-sampling`](shapley-sampling) — auditing sampled Shapley payments: exact estimator variances in a saturating game, permutation sampling is exactly efficient but stratified sampling has 6–8× lower variance (zero for equal contributors) at up to 7% budget error, antithetic reversal gives 1.6× lower RMSE, Hoeffding sample counts are 4–5× conservative.
- [`research/categorical-scores`](categorical-scores) — K-ary proper scores: exact worst-case regrets (`1−2p_min+‖p‖²` Brier, `‖p‖−p_min` spherical), spherical beats Brier by exactly `√K` per payment range at a uniform truth, and both bounded scores nearly ignore a dropped rare class (1e-4 vs 0.15 for log).
- [`research/multiclass-tangent-log`](multiclass-tangent-log) — bounded multiclass log score by quadratic tangent extension of `x ln x`: log-identical when all `r_j≥ε`, payment range exactly `1−ln ε` for any K, 63× Brier's rare-class incentive per range at ρ=10⁻³ with best cutoff ε=p_min, and a closed-form bounded cost for dropping a rare class.
- [`research/rank-elicitation`](rank-elicitation) — eliciting class rankings with linear rank-weight scores: strictly proper, adjacent-swap regret exactly `(p_i−p_j)(w_r−w_{r+1})`, beats Brier-derived rankings per unit range below gap `1/(K−1)` (250× at gap 10⁻³), equal spacing is optimal, top-k set costs the same as the mode, detection sample size unchanged.
- [`research/ordinal-scores`](ordinal-scores) — ranked probability score for ordered severities: exact regret `Σ(R_k−P_k)²`, shift over distance d costs `dε²` (Brier `2ε²`), pooling adjacent classes costs half of Brier's, threshold errors ≤ `√regret`, and per range RPS never beats Brier's incentive.
- [`research/tail-elicitation`](tail-elicitation) — jointly eliciting VaR and expected shortfall of benign drift: 0-homogeneous Fissler–Ziegel score minimised at (VaR, ES) with profile `ln ES`, ES alone non-elicitable (mixture 12.5 vs 10), exact regret formulas, ES-understatement of 30% detectable with ~1k samples at τ=0.95 while pinball is blind.
- [`research/mode-elicitation`](mode-elicitation) — eliciting the modal drift with the window loss `1[|y−r|>α]`: exact window-mass regret, best width `α=σ` (normal) / `γ/√3` (Cauchy), closed-form critical width `α*` above which bimodal drift has one α-mode, and 0 bias against contamination where the mean moves `5ε`.
- [`research/interval-scores`](interval-scores) — companion to interval-elicitation: Winkler interval score for drift-tolerance intervals: strictly proper for the α/2 and 1−α/2 quantiles, closed-form regret, optimal expected score `4sφ(z)/α`, width-misreport costs whose asymmetry flips with the level, and Cauchy drift where scores have no mean but paired differences are bounded.
- [`research/decision-scoring`](decision-scoring) — scoring verifiers whose report decides acceptance and whose outcome is seen only on acceptance: a truthful reserve exists iff the score centre `τ_s ≥ τ_d` (unique `c0 = 1+τ_d²−2τ_dτ_s`), plain Brier fails above `τ_d = ½`, recentring fixes it at rent `τ³/3`, exploration costs `1/ε` payment variance.
- [`research/interval-elicitation`](interval-elicitation) — paying for a drift tolerance interval: Winkler interval score is proper for the equal-tailed pair with exact regret (finite for Cauchy) but unbounded payment; the bounded width-plus-miss loss elicits the shortest interval (22–31% narrower on lognormal drift) at a 3–48× larger detection sample for α≥0.1.
- [`research/freivalds-float`](freivalds-float) — Freivalds' matrix-product check in floating point: rank-one corruption missed with probability exactly `erf(t/(√2F))` (worst case for fixed norm), `erf^m` for m probes, Rademacher probes stuck at ½ for any size, measured float32 noise floor, and `1/erf` grinding tries when the probe is hashed from the result.
- [`research/order-flow-liquidity`](order-flow-liquidity) — Glosten–Milgrom vs LMSR for a verification market: informed share fixes `b* = 1/(2 atanh m)` (verified to 2e-16), peak misspecification regret is scale-free and overconfident `b` costs 1.5–2.4× more, LMSR subsidy `→ b* ln 2` is the spread GM charges.
- [`research/conformal-tolerance`](conformal-tolerance) — calibrating slashing tolerances from honest runs: split-conformal false-slash law `(n+1-k)/(n+1)` is exact but only ~51–56% of calibration sets are ≤ α, PAC safety needs `n ≥ ln δ/ln(1−α)` (459 at 1%/1%), the margin is cheater hiding room (×1.26 at n=2000, Pareto-3), Gaussian plug-ins over-slash skewed drift up to 17×, pooled hardware calibration over-slashes the noisy class 4.8× and lets the quiet class hide 2.9×.
- [`research/layer-tolerance`](layer-tolerance) — per-layer vs total drift tolerances: a tolerance per layer admits `L·c(L,α)σ` vs `z_α√L σ` on the total (6.6× at L=16), the sum test ignores how a cheat is spread while the max test catches 2.7σ in one layer but lets 16σ through spread over all, and a union test caps the hidden budget at the sum test's.
- [`research/lmsr-fees`](lmsr-fees) — proportional fees on an LMSR verification market: exact no-trade band `[q/(1+f),(q+f)/(1+f)]`, price lag `q/(1+f)`, dead-market cliff `f ≥ 2a−1` (threshold `2 atanh f` at ½), exact fee revenue `f·b·ln((1−p₀)/(1−p_T))`, and a break-even fee that costs +38% Brier error.
- [`research/committee-sampling`](committee-sampling) — verifier committees from a finite pool: exact hypergeometric capture (zero once `m ≥ 2B+1`, up to 26× below binomial), exact size ≈0.90 of `ln(1/ε)/KL(½‖β)` vs Hoeffding up to 2.2× too large, stake-weighted draws depend only on Byzantine stake share (20 of 100 nodes at 4× stake capture a 41-committee w.p. ½), and the threshold safety/liveness trade-off.
- [`research/stopping-scores`](stopping-scores) — verifiers who choose when to stop checking under a proper score: optimal policy is a symmetric threshold (matches dynamic programming), closed-form accuracy `1/(1+((1−a)/a)^k)` and cost `k(2p_k−1)/(2a−1)`, participation `κ ≥ 4c/(2a−1)²`, and adaptive stopping matches fixed-sample accuracy with 2–3× fewer checks.
- [`research/verifier-cascades`](verifier-cascades) — sequential verifiers who see earlier verdicts herd: wrong-cascade probability exactly `(1−a)²/(a²+(1−a)²)`, cascade after `2/(a²+(1−a)²)` signals, accuracy capped at 0.845 (a=0.7) versus ≈1 for an independent majority, and a sealed first batch of ≥3 lifts the cap (0.875 at m=3, 0.949 at m=11); checked against Bayesian agents.
- [`research/calibration-hedging`](calibration-hedging) — calibration tests are gameable: an information-free Foster–Vohra/Blackwell hedger keeps expected ECE ≤ `√(K+1)(1/(2K)+1/√T)` against any outcome sequence (measured 0.016, Brier 0.254 vs 0 informed), so slashing must use a resolution-sensitive score; a Brier-gap test detects it in 100/100 runs.
- [`research/audit-staffing`](audit-staffing) — staffing the audit pool that stake-based deterrence needs: exact Erlang-C waiting (matches simulation), square-root staffing `R+1.06√R` (spare capacity 60% at R=5, 1.1% at 10⁴), stake–headcount optimum `G+S=√(wsG/(rd₀))` (cost 24 vs 77 at too little stake), and bursty audit demand delays 1.8× what Allen–Cunneen predicts.
- [`research/surprisingly-popular`](surprisingly-popular) — surprisingly-popular verdicts for rare-but-diagnostic signals: majority errs 0.84 on faulty jobs at n=101 when detection is 0.45, SP is an exact fraction threshold `v/(1−u+v)` (2.1×10⁻⁵ error, 47 votes for 10⁻³ vs Bayes 36), a wrong believed prior wrecks finite-n accuracy, a wrong believed detector breaks the limit, Byzantine breakdown at exactly 13.6%.
- [`research/position-rent`](position-rent) — sequential LMSR seat rents are conditional mutual information: they telescope to the maker's expected loss (≤ `b ln 2`), fall with position at the Chernoff rate, order only redistributes a fixed total, and entry costs make error fall only ~`1/b` in the subsidy.
- [`research/hetero-pipeline`](hetero-pipeline) — cutting a model across heterogeneous devices: exact greedy bottleneck partition (matches DP, with memory caps), `W/V ≤ T* < W/V + m·w_max/V`, speed-blind splitting costs exactly `v̄/v_min` (2.4× at σ=0.5), device order is the residual decision (sorts no better than arbitrary, swap search within 1.1%), memory inverse to speed costs 1.7×.
- [`research/straggler-backup`](straggler-backup) — waiting for the fastest k of n workers: exact order-statistic round times (Pareto mean finite iff `n−k+1 > 1/α`), exact SGD time-to-accuracy with a minimum k and an interior optimum (34× over wait-for-all at s=0, 1.12× at s=32), joint step tuning, and the exact selection bias of the speed filter with Horvitz–Thompson correction.
- [`research/rollout-commons`](rollout-commons) — rollout sharing in swarm RL as a public-goods game: Nash gives every agent a lone agent's learning level whatever N (total effort caps at `(a/c−1)/θ`) while efficient welfare is 4.5× at N=256; at θ=1 one agent generates; a Pigouvian matching subsidy (fraction `θ(N−1)/(1+θ(N−1))` of cost) implements the optimum; junk beats honesty iff `c−ε > p(τ+F)`.
- [`research/speed-race`](speed-race) — paying the first k of n finishers makes speed a contest: `π'(1)=(1−k/n)(H_n−H_{n−k})`, equilibrium speed `R(n−k)(H_n−H_{n−k})/(nc)`, exact round time `nc/((n−k)R)`; paying all finishers buys no speed, a budget favours winner-take-all until an accuracy floor `k_min` binds, and recruiting more workers barely speeds the round.
- [`research/race-heterogeneity`](race-heterogeneity) — paying the first k of n finishers when costs differ: proof of the `π'(1)` telescoping identity, exact exit rule (slow workers idle iff `c_s/c_f ≥ n_f/(n_f−k)`, never for `k ≥ n_f`), cost ratio 4 excludes slow workers for `k ≤ 3` of 4 fast, convex costs make headcount buy speed.
- [`research/sleeping-ledger`](sleeping-ledger) — a self-financed wealth ledger for specialist modules that sleep: total wealth conserved exactly, sleeping wealth frozen (one private ledger per regime), regret `≤ ln(1/π_i)/η + η·n_i` in the module's *own* awake time, entry of a module with wealth `ε` costs incumbents `ln(1/(1−ε))/η`; matches the regime oracle (0.1004 vs 0.1000) where forced-to-report Hedge sits at 0.30 and fixed share is worse.
- [`research/stale-rollouts`](stale-rollouts) — stale off-policy rollouts in swarm RL: exact importance-weight moments (`E w²=e^{δ²}`, ESS fraction `e^{−δ²}`), closed-form truncation bias/variance verified by simulation, best cap cuts shift-estimation MSE 1.9× at `δ=1` and ~750× at `δ=3`, and the staleness window `√ln(1/ρ)/drift`.
- [`research/precision-scores`](precision-scores) — scoring a verifier's vector report: the precision score `Σ⁻¹` is uniformly most detectable among quadratic Bregman scores (Cauchy–Schwarz), an isotropic score can need `(1+κ)²/(4κ)` times more tasks (25× at κ=100, power 0.29 vs 0.80), diagonal normalisation barely helps, estimated precision reaches 0.61 efficiency at `m=2d` draws.
- [`research/sleeping-incentives`](sleeping-incentives) — when may a module choose to sleep? The ledger pays exactly leave-one-out savings `ηW_A(1−p_i)S_i`; sleeping *after* seeing losses gains wealth `ηp_Bθ(1−θ)/2` (best 1/8) and takes the market in ~130 rounds with the router's loss unchanged; committed *before* serving it is a real saving; the deterring tax `ηp_B/2` is a fixed share that halves honest specialists in 6 rounds.
- [`research/topk-routing`](topk-routing) — top-k MoE routing as a capped Hedge market: price `min(1, λ w_i)` with exact KL projection, regret vs the best fixed k-set held under `k ln(N/k)/η+ηTk/2` in every run (41–58% of it), plain normalisation prices experts above 1 (282/300 rounds, loss understated 3.2×), and systematic sampling gives exactly k distinct experts with 8–14× lower variance.
- [`research/compute-shirking`](compute-shirking) — detecting skipped training steps from the loss alone: hidden compute is the loss curve inverted at the noise band (`f* = 1−(1+(e^q−1)/r)^{−1/β}`), grows as reducible loss falls, seed noise sets a floor no eval size removes, simulation matches within 1.5 points, and the loss test is worth ≈4.4 step audits at best.
- [`research/appeal-panels`](appeal-panels) — escalating jury appeal ladders with fee-priced appeals: an exact fee window makes error `∏(1−M_k)`, 11 jurors is the smallest deterring panel at prize 100, 3→11→27 errs 2.4e-4 at 5.8 expected seats vs 12.6% for one 7-seat panel, shared jury bias sets a floor `ρ(1−a)`.
- [`research/market-subspace-descent`](market-subspace-descent) — convergence of cost-function markets with partially informed traders as randomized subspace descent: maker loss telescopes to `½(e₀'Ae₀−e_T'Ae_T)`, closed-form slowdown from correlated securities (31→356 rounds at c=0.95, d=5), A-conjugate bundles restore `1−1/d` exactly, noisy-belief step size is an SGD step (running mean beats best constant step), LMSR simulation checks the local rates.
- [`research/collision-elicitation`](collision-elicitation) — eliciting the chance two honest runs of a nondeterministic op agree (Γ=Σp²) from run pairs: proper with exact regret, one run provably cannot, all-pairs U-statistic variance ~¼ of disjoint pairs, fleet of H hardware classes false-slashes strict equality at `1−H^{1−k}`, colluders' blind spot at ε=2Γ/(1+Γ).
- [`research/forgetting-law`](forgetting-law) — exact catastrophic-forgetting law for sequential training on random low-rank tasks: `(r/d)(1−r/d)(ρᵏ−λᵏ)` rises then vanishes, uniform module routing dilutes it but sharing wins on seen-plus-fresh loss, conflicting tasks leave a floor `2rτ²/d`.
- [`research/forgetting-audit`](forgetting-audit) — auditing skipped or partial continual-learning training from old-task losses: exact loss law (`ρ^{t−1}[(1−2m₁+m₂)(r/d)λᵏ+(r/d)ρ(ρᵏ−λᵏ)]`), a skipped task is ≥ `d/(d−r)`× worse but only clearly so inside a lag window, detecting a skipper takes ~`0.7/s²` jobs at any lag, a 10% step shortfall needs ~9600.
- [`research/merge-law`](merge-law) — merging independently trained modules on random low-rank tasks: exact residual `1−2amp+a²(mp+m(m−1)p²)`, optimal scale `1/(1+(m−1)p)` (averaging stalls at `(1−p)²`, unscaled sum diverges), harmonic `1/m` decay, parallel speedup only logarithmic (8.9× at m=16, d/r=8).
- [`research/replay-law`](replay-law) — exact expected forgetting under replay schedules in sequential training on random low-rank tasks: 2×2 matrix recursion matches simulation, one replay is best at the forgetting-peak lag yet removes only 25–42%, a fixed period beats random replay only below ~1.2–3 peak lags, halving forgetting needs rate 0.25 (`d=12, r=3`) to 0.015 (`d=128, r=1`).
- [`research/replay-memory`](replay-memory) — partial replay when only `m` of an old task's `r` examples are stored: exact 2×2 law interpolating no replay and full replay, best-step saving nearly linear in `m/r`, and at a fixed example budget a few large replays beat many small ones by only 0.1–6 points.
- [`research/router-impurity`](router-impurity) — task-aware routing when continual-learning tasks conflict: the long-run floor is `(2r/d)[τ_w²+τ_b²(1−Purity)]` with purity = 1 − the router's Gini impurity, random modules buy nothing, a perfect router removes `(m−1)/(K−1)` of the between-cluster floor, and a 10% routing error gives back 36%.
- [`research/stake-screening`](stake-screening) — can a bond tell reliable hardware from unreliable? Single crossing runs the wrong way: reliable rent `(θ_H−θ_L)(c+(1+ρ)s_H)/(1−θ_H)` is minimised at zero stake, a flat wage is optimal, and a deterrence stake scales rent by `(c+(1+ρ)s)/c`.
- [`research/outer-momentum`](outer-momentum) — DiLoCo-style outer momentum: `H` inner steps precondition the outer quadratic (`κ_H ≈ κ/H`), exact per-mode rates, optimal momentum falls 0.88 → 0.01 with `H` (fixed 0.7/0.9 Nesterov needs 4–8× more rounds), and the wallclock-optimal sync interval is `H* ≈ C` (sync cost in inner steps), worth ≈ `√C/2`.
- [`research/noisy-local-sgd`](noisy-local-sgd) — noise floor of DiLoCo-style local SGD: exact stationary loss `αV(1+β)/((1−β)s(2(1+β)−αs))`, independent of `H` at `α=1`, momentum free at equal effective step, near-linear speedup in `M`, and a noise-limited sync interval that grows as the floor target falls.
- [`research/partial-participation`](partial-participation) — local SGD when workers drop in and out: exact floors for participant averaging (`E[1/K|K≥1]`) and expected-count normalisation (stable iff `αsc<2`), at matched speed the fixed normaliser is quieter by `E[K]E[1/K]` at small steps, and reliable workers bias the fixed point unless inverse-propensity weighted.
- [`research/compressed-sync`](compressed-sync) — local SGD with compressed displacements: exact floor `(1+ω)` and stability limit `αs(1+ω/N)<2` for rand-k (more workers restore the step, not the floor), closed form for norm-scaled quantisation, and at equal bytes syncing less often beats compressing.
- [`research/delayed-outer`](delayed-outer) — overlapping communication in local SGD with `τ`-round-late outer updates: exact stable step `2 sin(π/(4τ+2))` (shrinks ~1/τ), double-root rate `τ/(τ+1)`, exact noise floor matched to simulation, free at small steps, wall-clock-optimal delay `⌈L/T_c⌉` (1.9–7.5× faster than no overlap).
- [`research/error-feedback-sync`](error-feedback-sync) — error feedback in compressed local SGD: exact stability limit `2(2−ρ)Nρ/(Nρ²+4(1−ρ))` and closed-form floor that returns to the uncompressed one at small steps (unbiased compression pays `1/ρ`), dropping is biased to the send-rate-weighted mean and the residual removes it at a variance price.
- [`research/heterogeneous-steps`](heterogeneous-steps) — weighting workers that ran different numbers of inner steps: exact floor `α²Σw²V/(r(2−r))`, worker information `I∞·tanh(λH/2)` saturates, optimal weights span ≤2× so equal weights lose ≤9/8 (measured ≤1.09) while step-proportional weights lose up to 1.76× per mode and 10.5× on three modes.
- [`research/robust-outer`](robust-outer) — median and trimmed-mean outer aggregation in local SGD: exact linear loop with floor and bias split, `π/2` variance price with no attackers, bounded worst-case bias `Φ(z)=1/(2(1−ε))` that more workers cannot average away but a longer inner loop shrinks.
- [`research/twin-transfer`](twin-transfer) — what a digital twin is worth for training a controller: exact LQ sim-to-real regret with a stability cliff, a validation blind spot (zero score gap yet mistrained control without probing), and a twin worth exactly `σ²/(vδ²)` real samples at any accuracy.
- [`research/randomized-twin`](randomized-twin) — domain randomization over a twin, exactly: cliff-limited width, quartic price when the twin was right, cliff pushed out ~15%, but uniform-average training is not minimax and the best width tracks the prior.
- [`research/twin-upkeep`](twin-upkeep) — keeping a digital twin in sync with a drifting plant: closed-form Kalman tracking variance and control regret (matched to simulation), a frozen twin loses half its edge in `ln 2/(2(1−ρ))` steps, probing never pays for one drifting parameter, and a two-parameter closed-loop blind direction gives a mean-field dither law `ν* ∝ q^{1/6}` that self-exciting certainty-equivalent control mostly makes unnecessary.
- [`research/twin-recalibration`](twin-recalibration) — re-identifying a drifting twin by dedicated experiments: closed-form interval `T*∝q_d^{−2/3}` with downtime, noise and drift regret exactly equal at the optimum, excess `∝q_d^{1/3}`, a 4× drift misjudgement costs ≤26%, but the second-order optimum destabilises 0.7–15% of cycles and a safe schedule is 37–200× worse than an always-on Kalman twin.
- [`research/twin-elicitation`](twin-elicitation) — what to pay a digital-twin provider for: cost-paid twins differ from Brier-paid ones, certainty-equivalence loss is quartic in spread, payment caps make the elicited twin jump at a closed-form threshold, and Brier mispays probing by 15×.
- [`research/radar-clutter-twin`](radar-clutter-twin) — what a Gaussian-clutter radar twin does to a CA-CFAR detector: exact false-alarm law under gamma texture (matched to simulation), 9–30× inflation at design 1e-4, 1.9–3.7 dB threshold repair, shared texture is harmless, and a real-cell calibration budget (median 1.1× at 1000 cells).
- [`research/cfar-family-twin`](cfar-family-twin) — does a more robust CFAR survive a Gaussian-clutter twin? CA, OS and log-t all inflate 8–44× at design 1e-4 (no detector escapes), a known-scale oracle inflates as much as CA (the test cell's own texture), and CA detects best at equal real Pfa after repair.
- [`research/handover-twin`](handover-twin) — when a collaborative robot should give up waiting for a human: `J'(τ)=S(τ)(w−c·h(τ))` makes exponential/Weibull simulated humans corner-only (never abort), 3.7% regret in the base case rising to 37–81% for heavy tails and cheap aborts, plug-in lognormal fit regret `1/n` (delta method within 11%).
- [`research/radar-detection-twin`](radar-detection-twin) — what a Gaussian-clutter radar twin promises about detection: exact Pfa/Pd laws for CA- and OS-CFAR in textured clutter, OS inflated like CA, an SNR shortfall (6.3 dB at ν=2) larger than the threshold repair, and no CA/OS ranking reversal.
- [`research/twin-audit`](twin-audit) — anytime-valid e-process audit of a digital twin in closed loop: peeking-safe (2.4% vs 58% false alarms), delay set by the controller's own excitation, regret before detection nearly independent of the gap, dither pays only for gross errors.
- [`research/occupancy-twin`](occupancy-twin) — what a too-clean sensor twin does to a mapper's commit error: exact real error via the Lundberg exponent (false-hit 0.05 twin vs 0.15 real turns 1e-3 into 1.45e-2, 14.5×), tempering restores the target at 1.4–16× latency, and the exponent needs several hundred real samples to estimate.
- [`research/demo-twin`](demo-twin) — cloning an expert from twin-logged demonstrations under actuator saturation: applied-action logs bias the clone even in a perfect twin (gain 0.58 vs 0.96, +9.8% cost), Stein closed form, quieter or looser twins help, narrow coverage fixes it, DAgger with applied labels is worse.
- [`research/twin-distillation`](twin-distillation) — cloning a privileged teacher from a digital twin into a noisy-sensor student: BC gain `gP/(P+s)` is set by the twin's noise-to-sensor ratio, a too-clean twin (¼ the noise) gives 0.36–1.11 excess cost vs 0.005–0.10, on-policy imitation is within 3.5% of optimal, cancellation with actuator error is accidental, and matching one real scalar by demonstration noise fixes it in two rounds.
- [`research/latency-twin`](latency-twin) — what a twin without sensor latency does to a tuned controller: exact stable-gain interval shrinks with delay, the zero-delay LQR gain tolerates one step (1.6× cost) and is unstable from two, retuned static gains cost 1.1–2.6×, wrong-delay predictors are mostly unstable, and delay identification needs `≈2z²(1+1/snr)` samples.
- [`research/targeted-observation-twin`](targeted-observation-twin) — UAV targeted observation planned in a forecast-ensemble twin: exact real error after twin-gain updates, a 2×-too-long correlation length claims −65% and delivers +28% (closed-form harm radius), 10 correct-model members already hurt, localisation and a fitted length scale (m≈50) repair it.
- [`research/workcell-twin`](workcell-twin) — what a deterministic-duration twin of a lab workcell promises: exact expected max of gamma branches, 1.86× promise miss with exponential branches (3× at the 95% quantile), station count under- or right-bought depending on how variability scales, and ~5 real durations fix the decision but ~50 the makespan.
- [`research/odometry-twin`](odometry-twin) — a twin tuned on one-step odometry noise cannot see drift: variance ratio exactly `1+(T−1)ρ`, relocalisation interval 3.4× too long (real miss 51.6% vs 5%), twin-tuned loop-closure gate recall 0.64 at T=200, closed-loop runaway, aliasing limits calibrated gates.
- [`research/grasp-twin`](grasp-twin) — how firmly to grip in a friction twin: closed-form (quadratic) optimum force under slip vs crush, a twin with spread 0.1 vs real 0.3 claims risk 1e-5 and delivers 0.069 (4× the optimum, 11.6× at 0.05), a zero-spread twin has no unique policy (real slip 0.50 to 0.015), width can cancel a known bias but not an unknown one, delta-method calibration budget.
- [`research/loopclosure-twin`](loopclosure-twin) — a SLAM loop-closure gate tuned in a too-clean odometry twin: exact chi-square gate costs and closed-form optimal gate, real noise 2× the twin's rejects 38% of true closures (claimed 2%) at 4.4× the claimed cost, pessimistic twins are not safe either, and ~59 ground-truthed closures repair it.
- [`research/loop-closure-twin`](loop-closure-twin) — a chi-square loop-closure gate tuned in a too-clean odometry twin: exact real error 2.1× the claim at drift-sd ratio 2, rejected genuine closures compound into lock-out (9–16× the ungated error, growing with horizon), the gate halves aliasing error when the twin is right but is 8× worse than no gate when it is not, and ~30 measured drifts make the repair safe.
- [`research/rendezvous-twin`](rendezvous-twin) — how often a mapping team should rendezvous when its twin's odometry drift has no bias: real error is exactly `√(1+ρ)` times the claim, cost-optimal interval solves a cubic, twin regret 0.19–34.6%, shared bias cancels in the pairwise frame (5.3%→0.10%), and repair needs a long-lag ground-truth fit.
- [`research/safety-twin`](safety-twin) — a corridor-keeping safety margin certified in a Gaussian-disturbance twin: same variance, heavy-tailed real gusts give 0.29 real failure at a claimed 0.01, needed margin 7.3× at `δ`=0.001 (`ν=3`), variance inflation misses by 24×, conformal needs `n≥1/δ`, a Hill tail fit from one-step data repairs it.
- [`research/twin-certification`](twin-certification) — can a twin shorten a real-world safety certification? Worst-case size of any `k ≤ c` rule is exactly `P(Bin(n,ε) ≤ c)`, so a twin prior cannot cut real trials at a fixed level (299 exact zero-failure trials; twin prior reaches 133 only at size 0.26); wrong twins inflate size or block certification.
- [`research/uav-energy-twin`](uav-energy-twin) — battery reserve of a UAV leg planned in a constant-wind twin: cruise speed nearly right (regret 0.1%) but the energy claim is low by exactly `artanh(x)/x` (3.5%) and a battery sized to it depletes in 50% of real flights; 5% depletion needs a 39% margin, and the twin speed stalls once wind spread exceeds `v−μ`.
- [`research/explanation-twin`](explanation-twin) — the cost a planner quotes from its own twin rollouts is optimistic by exactly `e_K·s` (winner's curse; 95% intervals cover 60% at K=20), fresh rollouts fix the sampling part at a selection-quality price, but not the twin's own model error `−e_K τ²/√(τ²+s²)`.
- [`research/retry-twin`](retry-twin) — how many retries a grasp needs when the twin treats attempts as independent: exact Beta-mixture failure law (power law `k^{−a}` vs geometric), twin budget 6 claims 0.004 and delivers 0.010–0.151, real budget 7–571, closed-form give-up rule, and a two-attempts-per-object repair that still needs a margin.
- [`research/dropout-twin`](dropout-twin) — a lidar twin with i.i.d. beam dropout certifies the thinnest detectable obstacle: real clustered dropout (same rate, lag-1 correlation 0.6) misses 41× more at the certified width and the range claim is 4× too long (exact Markov miss law, `p·q^(m−1)`); a fitted persistence recovers the median width but needs a margin.
- [`research/selection-twin`](selection-twin) — the best of K controllers picked in a noisy twin: winner's claim optimistic by exactly `τ²c_K/√(v²+τ²)` (`c_K`=E max of K normals), under a fixed rollout budget more candidates can make the deployed one worse (best K=5–22), an exact-plant LQ twin's claim falls below what any gain can achieve, and screen-then-verify cuts claim bias 4.5×.
- [`research/twin-evaluation`](twin-evaluation) — evaluating a robot policy with a biased twin as a control variate: unbiased whatever the twin's bias (+0.700 twin-only vs +0.001), exact variance `σ²((1−ρ²)/n+ρ²/N)`, `ρ=η` for a faithful twin but `√η` for a noise-free one (1.33× vs 2.0× real-sample gain), worth-it threshold `c_T/c_R<(1−√(1−ρ²))²/ρ²` (0.072 at ρ=0.5), and 86–125 vs 164 real episodes per policy to rank correctly when twin bias favours the wrong one.
- [`research/twin-pilot`](twin-pilot) — how big a pilot decides whether a biased twin is worth using: threshold `ρ*=2√r/(1+r)` with a gain linear in `ρ−ρ*`, so wrong calls are frequent but cheap (regret 0.004–0.016 at `ρ`=0.5), decision-regret law `f·slope·(1−ρ*²)²/(2m)` (ratio 0.63→1.00 for m=10→160), plug-in allocation `≈0.27–0.30/m`, and an adaptive two-stage design that stays unbiased (|bias| ≤ 0.002) with 0.946–0.955 coverage.
- [`research/crn-twin`](crn-twin) — seed-paired (common random numbers) policy comparison in a twin: correlation is exactly lost to random-stream desynchronisation (flat score 0.95→0.77, terminal score 0.36→0.001 at q=0.05→0.3), a sizing trap (power 0.11–0.85 vs nominal 0.90), and counter-keyed draws restore ρ=1.
- [`research/timestep-twin`](timestep-twin) — what overshoot a controller tuned in a fixed-step twin really has: exact per-step decay laws for explicit/semi-implicit/implicit Euler, damping bias flips sign at `ζ=1/√2` (explicit, implicit), a 10% twin target delivers 0.105–0.192 real overshoot (implicit/semi-implicit) or 0.036–0.091 (explicit), error first order in `h`, Richardson repair.
- [`research/scenario-twin`](scenario-twin) — a too-narrow scenario generator in a twin makes rare-failure estimates silent: reweighted variance finite iff `s²>½`, 0.6× width claims 2e-7 for a real 1.2e-3 and reports zero failures in all runs, optimal width ≈1.05·`a` needs 38× fewer runs than plain Monte Carlo, and a defensive real-prior mixture is finite but no better.
- [`research/filter-twin`](filter-twin) — an estimator tuned in a twin with wrong noise levels: exact real MSE of the twin's Kalman gain, optimistic claim when the twin is too quiet (+121% at 10×) vs pessimistic when too noisy, no instability cliff, and a Mehra innovation retune with regret ∝ 1/n.
- [`research/control-twin`](control-twin) — a controller designed in a twin with the wrong actuator gain: exact real LQR cost, an instability band the twin does not warn about, closed-loop non-identifiability without dither, and a dithered retune with matched regret law.
- [`research/saturation-twin`](saturation-twin) — a limit-free twin of an unstable plant with a saturating actuator overstates mean time to loss of control by 6,295× at noise sd 1 (1.5×10⁶ vs 245 steps), an exponential-in-1/s² gap (slope ≈3.7 real vs ≈12.7–13.2 twin), though both rank the deadbeat gain best.
- [`research/quantization-twin`](quantization-twin) — a Kalman filter tuned in an ideal-sensor twin, run on a quantizing sensor: twin gain costs +11% (`R`=1, `D`=4) to +64% (`R`=0.01), Sheppard `R+D²/12` retune is within 1% of best, but its MSE claim under-predicts by up to 43% once the step exceeds the state's scale.
- [`research/ladder-twin`](ladder-twin) — splitting a simulation budget across twin fidelities (multilevel Monte Carlo): smooth quantity saves 3.6×–897× (rates α=1, β=2), a failure probability only β=1 so multilevel loses at loose targets (0.4×) and reaches 7.7× at 1e-4; equal samples per level cost 156×/3.9× the optimum.
- [`research/finetune-twin`](finetune-twin) — fine-tuning a twin-pretrained policy on the real plant: exact SGD risk, a twin start saves 64–67% of real steps at 0.6× the cold error but is negative transfer beyond 1×, and twin error decays only as `b²/(4eηk)` (flat directions inherited: 13 vs 1,541 steps at equal initial loss).
- [`research/horizon-twin`](horizon-twin) — how far ahead a controller planned in a wrong-pole twin should look: exact real cost per horizon is non-monotone when the twin overstates the pole (1.81 at H=2 vs 3.97 at H=60, optimum 1.48), the twin's own claim ranks horizons backwards, exact two-step stability edge, and a Riccati terminal cost removes the effect.
- [`research/mix-twin`](mix-twin) — mixing cheap biased twin samples with scarce real ones: exact optimal weight, twin worth capped at `σ²/δ²` real samples, equal pooling helps iff `δ²<a+b`, and estimating the bias from data is costly (plug-in worse than real-only for large bias).
- [`research/queue-twin`](queue-twin) — how much load a shared lab instrument carries when its twin assumes exponential service: exact Pollaczek–Khinchine wait off by `(1+c²)/2`, twin admits ρ=0.667 vs real 0.444 (`c²=4`) or 0.133 (`c²=25`), tails 11.7× worse, infinite mean wait for Pareto index < 2, and a plug-in `c²` repair that violates the SLA 70–89% of the time.
- [`research/smooth-twin`](smooth-twin) — smoothing a hard contact edge to make a twin trainable: real gradient is zero, twin ascent is trapped below `τ_c=0.105` and misses the edge above `R/(8cm)`, annealing crosses the trap, and the overshoot `≈τ ln(R/(2cmτ))` is an implicit safety margin capped at 0.168.
- [`research/fusion-twin`](fusion-twin) — how many redundant sensors a twin with independent errors says a robot needs: exact equicorrelated fused variance, `n_eff < 1/ρ` and a floor `σ√ρ`, twin says 16 sensors vs real 30 (ρ=0.03) or none (ρ≥0.0625), 3σ gate false-alarms 8.6× too often, and a plug-in ρ repair that misses the target about half the time.
- [`research/autocorr-twin`](autocorr-twin) — how much one long digital-twin run is worth when its output is autocorrelated: exact AR(1) run-mean variance, M/M/1 wait inflation 9.7×–363× (ρ=0.5–0.9), naive 95% intervals cover 9–53%, false SLA certification 26% vs 4.8% with batch means, and batch means fail when batches are shorter than the correlation time.
- [`research/warmup-twin`](warmup-twin) — a twin started in the wrong state: exact AR(1) warm-up bias and MSE-optimal deletion (≈ one relaxation time, ≤1.5× gain), M/M/1 empty-start bias (−21% at ρ=0.9, n=500; 1% at 10⁴), and a negative result: MSER-5 truncation leaves more bias and more false SLA certifications than fixed deletion.
- [`research/nlos-twin`](nlos-twin) — a twin with Gaussian range noise vs real non-line-of-sight bias: exact rms law `GDOP·√(σ²+p(2−p)m²)` (4.5–7.3× the twin's error, zero mean), a predictable bias for a blocked side, anchor counts 4 → 28–46 for a 1 m / 95% corridor, and a trimming fix that costs 24% when NLOS is absent.
- [`research/stop-twin`](stop-twin) — when to stop a twin run counting rare failures: sequential Wald stopping halts on zero failures (90% of runs at `p`=10⁻³, coverage 9.6%), inverse sampling to `m` failures gives exactly unbiased `(m−1)/(N−1)` with relative sd `≈1/√(m−2)` and an exact interval (95.0–95.5%), while a fixed run planned for the wrong `p` delivers 63–200% half-width.
- [`research/terrain-twin`](terrain-twin) — stopping distance certified by a twin with independent per-cell friction: exact incomplete-gamma law (matches braking simulation), 100 twin cells certify 1.17× the mean distance but real terrain with 1–10 patches exceeds it 44%–18% (claimed 0.1%), finer cells are worse, the mean is nearly right, and the measured patch count repairs it.
- [`research/input-twin`](input-twin) — a twin fitted to finite real data: exact delta-method error `√(1+(2−ρ)²)/((1−ρ)√n)` of its steady-state wait, data needed grows as `(1−ρ)⁻²` (22 100 per stream for 10% at ρ=0.9), exact instability probability, false SLA certification 36–46% fixed by bootstrap/posterior bounds (delta bound under-covers).
- [`research/budget-twin`](budget-twin) — real data or more simulation for a fitted twin: relative variance `a(ρ)/n + b(ρ)/m` (matches simulation), one real pair ≈ 2 simulated jobs at any load, optimal real-data share 0.57–0.97 by cost ratio, and a simulation-only interval whose real coverage falls 0.79→0.17 as the run lengthens while the input-aware one stays 0.92–0.95.
- [`research/beam-twin`](beam-twin) — approach speed certified by a lidar twin with independent beam dropout vs real whole-scan fades: exact scan-product miss law, real miss ~100× the level at the twin's speed, speed off 10–30% (probabilities off by orders of magnitude), sign reverses when several returns are needed, repaired by a few hundred calibration scans.
- [`research/tilt-twin`](tilt-twin) — importance sampling of a rare twin failure: exact mean-shift variance, 10⁸→535 runs for 10% error at p=1e-6, a narrow shift target (2× the optimum is 1.7·10⁵× worse, 0% coverage) and effective sample size as the only warning.
- [`research/closedloop-twin`](closedloop-twin) — a twin identified from logs of the deployed controller: held-out error is 1.00σ² at every dither level while the predicted pole under a new gain has sd `(σ/√n)√((k−k')²/τ²+1/v)` (0.037→0.82), no dither gives a confident wrong twin (b̂=−0.22 vs +1), point-rule false stability certification 3–48% vs 0.1–4.7% for the OLS bound.
- [`research/doppler-twin`](doppler-twin) — radar ego-velocity from Doppler returns: a static-world twin picks least squares, but with moving targets its variance is exactly `1+ετ²` times the claim (95% interval covers 48.7% at ε=0.1, τ=10) and the median wins on a band of ε (0.006–0.97 at τ=10; never at τ=1).
- [`research/alias-twin`](alias-twin) — a radar twin with no Doppler folding: the twin-tuned ego-speed estimator breaks at `v_u−2σ` with an exact closed-form bias (saturating at one fold off), and a wrapped-cost search unfolds it iff azimuths are diverse, with a confusion probability `Φ(−√E₁/2σ)` matching Monte Carlo.
- [`research/validate-twin`](validate-twin) — validating a twin's rare-failure rate against real trials: an exact difference test "passes" a 3×-wrong twin 81% of the time at 500 trials, rejecting a 2× error needs ~11 real failures (any q), and an equivalence test needs ~20/58/178 failures for margins 2/1.5/1.25 (~20/q trials).
- [`research/intent-twin`](intent-twin) — goal inference tuned against a simulated human: exact real error `1/(1+r^h)` of a commit threshold, 3.9×–165× over target for a too-rational twin, 2–4.5× slower for a too-irrational one, population floor `P(p<½)`, and calibration from n real cues misses the target 14–42% of the time.
- [`research/sync-twin`](sync-twin) — sensor clock offset and jitter a twin does not simulate: exact added variance `2σ²[1−(1+s²/ℓ²)^{−½}e^{−δ²/2(ℓ²+s²)}]`, a synchronised-clock fusion design is `1+ag/(b(a+b))`× its claim (+44% at 0.3ℓ) and worse than one sensor beyond 0.225ℓ, better delayed sensors hurt more, clock randomisation fixes it.
- [`research/eiv-twin`](eiv-twin) — a twin gain fitted from a noisy logged input is attenuated by exactly the reliability ratio `λ` (0.800 vs 0.802 simulated), more data makes the twin's own 95% interval worse (covers the true gain 53%→0% for n=20→200) with no residual warning, a feedforward controller overshoots by exactly `1/λ`, and known-noise or instrument repairs cost variance and fail at small n.
- [`research/deadband-twin`](deadband-twin) — an actuator deadband the twin lacks: real loop rests at exactly `d/k` while the twin goes to 0, tolerance `ε` needs `k ≥ d/ε`, a fitted linear gain is `2Q(d/s)` (excitation-dependent, small-signal gain 0), and bisection compensation leaves exact `(d−d̂)/k` or a `(d̂−d)/(2−k)` chatter (8 probes: 0.784 → 0.0005).
- [`research/deadband-noise-twin`](deadband-noise-twin) — follow-up to `deadband-twin`: measurement noise flips the linear twin's error from 27× optimistic (s=0.01) to conservative (0.37× at s=0.05), dither helps only without noise, and mild under-compensation beats the exact width under noise (single-seed simulations).
- [`research/backlash-twin`](backlash-twin) — a rigid-drivetrain twin says the PI loop converges but gear backlash makes the real loop hunt at 1.3–2.9× the gap (exactly ∝ h); describing-function prediction 7–10% high, and bistable starts can hide it.
- [`research/slew-twin`](slew-twin) — a twin without an actuator rate limit is exact only for `x₀ ≤ r/k`; beyond it the real loop overshoots (0.90 of `x₀` at `x₀/r=10⁴`) and settles in O(amplitude) steps, with an empirical overshoot threshold `2n(2n−1)` for `k=1/n`.
- [`research/friction-twin`](friction-twin) — a viscous-only twin never stops (6.5–17.7 s to reach 1e-3…1e-9 vs 1.95 s real) and its fitted gain `b + c·E[v]/E[v²]` depends on the logged speed range; fitted on [1,3] it overestimates stopping distance ×1.8–17 below and underestimates above `v*`=7.3, no single viscous gain beats 0.87 worst error over [0.05,3], affine fit recovers exactly.
- [`research/stiction-twin`](stiction-twin) — a twin with the right sliding friction says the PID loop converges, and with no stiction it does; a breakaway excess `Fs−Fc` makes the real loop stick-slip hunt at ≈0.5–0.9 `ΔF/kp` (independent of `Fc`), and short runs can mistake a long stick for convergence.
- [`research/thrust-twin`](thrust-twin) — a controller that inverts a linear-thrust twin meets a quadratic thrust curve: realised acceleration is a+a²/(4g), so climbs overshoot more (0.046→0.095) and descents less, and steps below p≈0.5 hardly notice.
- [`research/invthrust-twin`](invthrust-twin) — exact square-root thrust inverse vs the linear one on a quadratic-thrust vehicle: up/down overshoot gap 3.00→0.09 points with the right model, but only 3.67→1.65 when the thrust exponent is misidentified (2.5 vs 2).
- [`research/lag-twin`](lag-twin) — a twin with an instantaneous actuator is stable at any gain; the real loop with lag τ is stable iff τ<kd/kp=2ζ/ωn, hunts at the twin's own ωn, decays no faster than 1/(3τ) for any gains, and the real-optimal bandwidth is ≈0.26–0.46 of the limit.
- [`research/flex-twin`](flex-twin) — a rigid-body twin certifies every PD bandwidth; with load-side sensing the real two-mass loop is unstable above `ε_c ≈ δ/ζ` of the flexible-mode frequency (exactly independent of mass ratio, unstable at any gain with no modal damping), step-response validation misses it, and the certified bandwidth inherits modal-damping error one-for-one.
- [`research/coupling-twin`](coupling-twin) — a per-axis twin of a two-axis loop certifies gains that actuator coupling destabilises: stable-gain limit `2/(1+√p)` not 2 (a 10% margin fails at `c=1/9`), twin-deadbeat rate is `√|p|` not 0, and a fitted diagonal gain `1+cρ` depends on log correlation while the full fit's variance grows as `1/(1−ρ²)`.
- [`research/windup-twin`](windup-twin) — a twin with an unlimited actuator is exact for a PI step only while `kp·x0 ≤ U`; beyond it the integrator winds up to `x0²/(2U)`, overshoot rises from 21% to 97% and settling from 4.9 to 944, loads `d ≥ U` are never rejected, and the twin-tuned faster gains settle slower (clamping recovers most).
- [`research/impact-twin`](impact-twin) — a fixed-step twin of a bouncing ball: the true rest time `(v1/g)(1+e)/(1−e)` is hypersensitive to `e` (`2/(1−e²)`), the twin's apparent restitution and settling time are off by O(dt) with scheme-dependent sign (−20% to +4% at dt=10⁻², e=0.9), and calibrating `e` on the first bounce is not a reliable fix.
- [`research/sample-twin`](sample-twin) — a continuous-time twin certifies PD at every gain; the real zero-order-hold loop is stable iff `kp·T/2 < kd < 2/T` (`ωn·T < min(4ζ, 1/ζ)`), overshoot is 5× the twin's at `ωn·T` = 1.2, raising `ωn` past `ωn·T` ≈ 1 slows settling, and deadbeat (2 samples) is the real speed limit.
- [`research/gravity-twin`](gravity-twin) — a gravity-free twin certifies PD with zero error; the real pendulum sags to the root of kp(θ*−θ)=g sin θ (0.18 rad at kp=4, falling only as ~1/kp), cannot hold upright unless kp>g (basin 0.12 rad at 1.02g), and a 10%-off gravity feedforward leaves a sag linear in the error.
- [`research/jitter-twin`](jitter-twin) — a twin fitted to the sensor-delay histogram is blind to its persistence: i.i.d. jitter at the mean delay barely moves the mean-square gain limit (1.00 for {0,2}), but with the marginal fixed, lag-1 autocorrelation 0.9 cuts it to 0.67 (0.9 certified by the twin is unstable from ρ=0.435) and inflates variance 2.4×; almost-sure stability (≈1.84) hides it.
- [`research/drag-twin`](drag-twin) — a linear-drag twin of a quadratic-drag vehicle: coast-down time and terminal speed are off by fit-speed ratios, least-squares fit gives `k = 3c·vmax/4`, and the safe speed under a stopping limit is mis-stated by −21% to +41% with sign set by the fit speed.
- [`research/slip-twin`](slip-twin) — a kinematic-bicycle twin of a car ignores tyre slip: steady-turn radius is off by exactly `1 + Kv²/L` (twin 4–63% too small, half at the characteristic speed), calibrating `K` fixes that but both twins certify steering gains stable at every speed while 11 of 30 real gain pairs lose stability below 60 m/s.
- [`research/shutter-twin`](shutter-twin) — a global-shutter twin of a rolling-shutter camera ignores target motion: a moving rectangle is sheared and stretched by exact closed forms, a twin-trained orientation estimate is biased by half the edge tilt (1.79° at 1000 px/s), and one frame of a known-size target identifies pose and velocity (0.027° and 16 px/s at 0.25 px noise). Stylised.
- [`research/sweep-twin`](sweep-twin) — a snapshot-scan twin of a spinning lidar ignores motion during the sweep: length biased by exactly `vτ/2` (0.5 m at 10 m/s), parallel walls converge by `vτ/2π·(1/d₁+1/d₂)`, one scan gives speed to 0.13 m/s, yaw scales bearings by `−κβ/(1+κ)`, acceleration biases odometry by `a·t_f` without accumulating.
- [`research/gnss-twin`](gnss-twin) — a white-noise satellite-positioning twin vs a receiver with correlated multipath: exact HDOP `√(4/n)` and bias map `2b/n`, averaging loses the `1/√T` gain (95% radius covers 33% at T=10, 5% at T=100), and a residual integrity test tuned on the twin false-alarms 21% at equal multipath. Stylised.
- [`research/inertia-twin`](inertia-twin) — point-mass twin of a rolling body ignores rotational inertia: acceleration overstated by exactly `1+k`, slip threshold `k tanα/(1+k)` invisible, coast distance 40–100% off, one calibration cannot fix a mixed fleet.
- [`research/lens-twin`](lens-twin) — a pinhole twin of a radially distorted camera mis-ranges the floor by exactly `1/(1+k₁h²/R²)` (+8.1% at 1 m for `k₁=−0.3`), a twin-trained braking policy stops 9 cm short of 1 m, focal calibration trades the near-field error for a far-field one, and one grid image identifies `k₁`.
- [`research/blur-twin`](blur-twin) — an instantaneous-exposure twin of a finite-exposure camera certifies detection at every speed; a moving bar is an exact trapezoid (peak `C·min(1, w/d)`), a twin-tuned peak detector has a hard speed limit `wC/(σz)`, and a blur-matched box filter raises it quadratically (5–55×).
- [`research/compass-twin`](compass-twin) — a twin with an ideal magnetometer hides hard/soft-iron heading error: exact peaks `asin(β/B)` and `asin(|1−r|/(1+r))`, a compass-held square misses by exactly `2Lβ/B`, and swing calibration error grows ×12 at a 90° arc. Stylised.
- [`research/gyro-twin`](gyro-twin) — a gyro twin calibrated on white noise hides the bias random walk: heading variance ratio exactly `1+(t/t*)²` (2.33 at 100 s, 134 at 1000 s), the twin's re-alignment interval is 24× too long (10 000 s vs 416 s), a single-point Allan fit is right at one horizon only, and dead-reckoning range is over-stated 1.25–1.74×. Stylised.
- [`research/grid-twin`](grid-twin) — a grid twin over-states every route by a heading-dependent factor that cell size does not shrink (8-connected: mean `8(√2−1)/π = 1.0548`, worst 1.0824 at 22.5°), calibrating the mean leaves −5.2% to +2.6%, and the twin ranks two routes (lengths 1 and 1.03) the wrong way round 19% of the time; rotating the map flips a fixed pair for 31% of orientations.
- [`research/roundtrip-twin`](roundtrip-twin) — zero-wind twin of a UAV out-and-back leg: wind does not cancel (real/twin energy `1/(1−(w/v)²)`), the best airspeed moves to `v²=w²+√(w⁴+1)`, and a one-leg-calibrated twin is off by exactly `±w/v` with the sign of the leg flown.
- [`research/thermal-twin`](thermal-twin) — a constant-gain twin of a motor holding a load certifies every load: magnet derating makes holding a positive thermal feedback with a fold at `λ=4/27` (real temperature 1.33–2.25× the twin's near it, no hold above it, runaway in `≈4π/(9√ε)` thermal time constants), short tests cannot see it, the twin overstates holdable torque 1.1–1.6×, and refitting a constant gain does not remove the fold.
- [`research/range-twin`](range-twin) — a twin whose sensor reads at any distance vs a range-limited one: exact step-count law (49 vs 4021 steps from 1000R), PI overshoot neither bound nor lower bound (4.1× at 1000R), exact scale invariance, and the gain fitted from logs of a given extent.
- [`research/replicate-twin`](replicate-twin) — one long twin run or many replications under a start-up transient: exact AR(1) MSE and t-interval coverage (single run minimises MSE; replication costs 1.07×–2.2×; 92% coverage at MSE-optimal deletion for short replications), batch means not worse, preliminary M/M/1 coverage 6–84%.
- [`research/warmstart-twin`](warmstart-twin) — cold-start bias of short twin runs: exact AR(1) bias/variance/warm-up deletion, M/M/1 empty-start bias constant `ρ/(1−ρ)³`, negligible at valid-interval lengths but −5.6 of 9 at 50 jobs; MSER-5 did not help.
- [`research/twin-screen`](twin-screen) — shortlisting K designs in a twin and verifying the top m on the real plant under a fixed trial budget: twin-only value exactly `ρc_K`, best m falls from 8 to 2 as ρ rises 0.6→0.95 (K=50, B=20), and a useless twin still gains 0.12 from budget concentration alone. Stylised.
- [`research/twin-shrinkage`](twin-shrinkage) — pulling a real-plant parameter estimate toward its twin (James–Stein): exact risk `d−(d−2)²E[1/(d−2+2K)]`, never worse in total for d≥3, perfect twin risk 2 at any d, worth `n+σ²/τ²` real samples at large d, but one mismatched parameter takes ≈`d/4` noise variances (25.5 at d=100) unless shrinkage is blockwise (1.65 at blocks of 5, d=30).
- [`research/slosh-twin`](slosh-twin) — a rigid twin of a cart carrying liquid predicts zero slosh: minimum-time move leaves 10.7 cm (10× tolerance), slosh-safe move is 55% slower, and a full-tank frequency calibration fails at low fill (28 s vs 8.7 s recalibrated; tuning-free bound 24.7 s).
- [`research/shaper-twin`](shaper-twin) — input-shaped moves of a liquid-carrying cart: ZV/ZVD residual `sin(πε/2)`, `sin²(πε/2)` exactly multiplies the bang-bang residual, so a stale frequency calibration that breaks the null-tuned move (28 s) is harmless to a 5.9 s ZV move; closed-form tolerated error and a fill-band design that spends the tolerance on a shorter shaper.
- [`research/mount-twin`](mount-twin) — a radar twin assuming the sensor is body-aligned: planar dead reckoning is exactly the true path rotated by the mount yaw (error `2 sin(e/2)·|displacement|`, bounded, zero after loops, so loop-closure validation is blind), closed-form rotation fit with std `σ/√Σ|p|²`, but a pitched mount drifts vertically `laps·2πR sin e`.
- [`research/twin-control-variate`](twin-control-variate) — certifying a policy with a biased digital twin: exact twin/plant cost correlation, optimal twin-to-real rollout split and speed-up (matched by simulation), bias first order but correlation second order in model error so the replayable-disturbance fraction caps the twin's value, and twin-only intervals cover ~0%.
- [`research/twin-multifidelity`](twin-multifidelity) — estimating a controller's real cost with a biased digital twin as a control variate: cost correlation in closed form (`0.985 → 0.472` as the twin's gain error grows while its bias grows 300×), exactly `λ²` under partial disturbance replay, optimal twin/real ratio `√(c_rρ²/(c_t(1−ρ²)))` and break-even twin price, simulated variance ratio 0.228 vs 0.225 predicted.
- [`research/twin-monitor`](twin-monitor) — anytime-valid monitoring of a deployed twin: e-process on closed-loop residuals (2.3% vs 51.6% false alarms under peeking), rate = twin-transfer score gap, an invisible blind line `Δa = kΔb` that needs dither `v`, and when dithering pays.
- [`research/compressed-outer`](compressed-outer) — compressing DiLoCo reports: unbiased compression multiplies the contraction (`c = 1+ω/M`, stable iff `αsc<2`, participation is `ω = 1/p−1`), dithered quantisation only adds `Δ²/12` and keeps stability, and at fixed bandwidth quantisation has an interior optimum near 1.3 bits per coordinate (~4× below 8-bit reports) while sparsification favours few full reports.
- [`research/sparse-outer-sync`](sparse-outer-sync) — sending a fraction `p` of the outer update: unbiased rescaling costs `1/p` in noise floor, error feedback costs only `1+αs(1/p−1)` at small steps but caps contraction at `√(1−p)`; exact closed form, stability limits, horizon losses.
- [`research/outer-delay`](outer-delay) — overlapping the DiLoCo sync by applying the outer step `τ` rounds late does not pay: stable iff `αs < 2 sin(π/(4τ+2))`, best rate `1−2 sin(π/(4τ+2))/κ_H` (exactly `κ_H/(κ_H+1)` at `τ=1`), momentum is useless once `τ≥1` (69 → 1388 rounds at `κ_H=100`), hidden-sync gain ≤ `(τ+1) sin(π/(4τ+2)) ≤ 1` (search: 1.5–4.9× slower than blocking averaging), fixed if the fresh pseudo-gradient has weight `>½`.
- [`research/private-data-market`](private-data-market) — buying private data with a telescoping log-score market: payment equals information, best Gaussian-mechanism noise in closed form, participation iff `κa<1` so precision is capped at `λ/(cΔ²)` for any number of contributors, and early arrivals over-collect (1–22% welfare loss).
- [`research/decision-markets`](decision-markets) — conditional markets that route between models: inverse-propensity Brier is proper even when the router reads the report, exploration costs exactly `1+(K−1)e^{ω²/2τ²}` in payment variance (probit: infinite for `τ ≤ ω`), and a stake gap distorts the reported gap by a closed-form fixed point with second-order harm.
- [`research/milestone-schedules`](milestone-schedules) — milestone payments without enforcement: a split pre-payment needs only the pooled relationship value (`C/(W_r+W_w)` vs `P/W_r`, `C/W_w`), and front-loading makes the milestone count logarithmic, `⌈ln(1+(V−C)/W)/ln(V/C)⌉` (17 vs 100 at W=1).
- [`research/gust-twin`](gust-twin) — a twin whose wind gusts are iid per step with the logged variance: real position variance is `s²(a+k_d)/(k_d k_p(a²+a k_d+k_p))`, the twin's is 141× smaller at `a`=1, `dt`=0.01 and scales as `dt`, its 1e-3 margin is exceeded 78% of the time, and a spectrum-matched white twin is always conservative.
- [`research/runlength-twin`](runlength-twin) — real test-run length sized in a wrong-pole twin: exact autocorrelated-cost variance, 3.8× too short (coverage 0.69 vs 0.95) or 2.1× too long depending on the real closed-loop pole, and a pilot-run repair that under-sizes half the time.
- [`research/aliasing-twin`](aliasing-twin) — what a radar twin with unlimited unambiguous velocity over-certifies: exact wrapped-Gaussian bias (`−V` at `μ=V`) and MSE with a `bias²` floor so averaging widens the gap (7.9× → 2.1×10³ at `μ=0.8V`, n=1→10⁴), safe speed `≈V+sΦ⁻¹(tol/2V)`, and prior-unwrapping failure `2Φ(−V/√(s²+s_p²))` needing prior error ≤ 0.26V.
- [`research/twin-ladder`](twin-ladder) — which rungs of a multi-fidelity twin ladder to run: exact nested-estimator variance, a cheaper rung pays iff `ρ₂/ρ₁ > 2√r/(1+r)` (step correlation, 0.943 for halved cost), using all rungs can be worse than none (1.41× real-only at q=0.9) while the best subset gets 0.83, and a timestep-ladder plant shows 0.156 of real-only variance (0.204 single twin) collapsing to the cheapest twin when disturbance replay is only 90%.
- [`research/module-entry`](module-entry) — admitting new modules to a Hedge/LMSR router: regret certificates obey a Kraft identity `Σ e^{−c_i}=1`, so uniform entry `1/(n+1)` is the unique minimax rule; prior-optimal shares `P_j/S_j` cost `H(P)`; the price of entry is exactly `ln(1/π)/η` independent of the quality gap.
- [`research/skill-score-normalisation`](skill-score-normalisation) — Brier skill scores normalised by the realised base rate are improper: exact optimal report, `≈ −(1−2p)/n` bias, cross-task leakage through the pooled baseline, and a leave-one-out fix.
- [`research/cfmm-markets`](cfmm-markets) — constant-product AMMs as cost-function prediction markets: worst-case loss `C0` never attained, informed-trader profit exactly `C0(1−n·GM(π))` (ratio to LMSR → `ln n`, crossover at n=e), polynomial price tails (p=0.999 costs 3.4× LMSR), exact regret identity, no forgiveness gain under regime switches.
- [`research/multi-observation-elicitation`](multi-observation-elicitation) — eliciting drift *variance* from pairs of independent draws: exact pair score (excess loss `(r−σ²)²`), degree table (mean² 2, third moment 3, σ⁴ 4), audit cost `((z_α+z_β)/ρ)²(κ+1)/2` pairs, robust Gini score beats it for heavy tails, all-pairs pooling saves `(κ+1)/(κ−1)`, correlated draws bias the target to `σ²(1−ρ)`.
- [`research/multipath-twin`](multipath-twin) — free-space radar twin vs two-ray ground-bounce lobing: detection fraction exactly 2/3 at the free-space range edge, real detects 44% beyond it, a two-ray twin with height error δh disagrees ≈`4h_rδh/(λr)` and is worse than free space at short range, a height-jitter ensemble scores Brier 0.119 vs 0.197 deterministic. Stylised.
- [`research/glint-twin`](glint-twin) — point-target radar twin of two unresolved scatterers under ideal monopulse: real mean is exactly the strong scatterer and median exactly the power centroid, outside-span probability `arccos(r)/π`, a variance-matched Gaussian twin is 27× too optimistic at a 3σ gate (r=0.9), averaging looks floors at bias `2r²/(1+r²)` where the twin predicts `√(var/N)`, and the median beats the mean for r≥0.477. Stylised.
- [`research/sidelobe-twin`](sidelobe-twin) — single-cell radar twin vs a real matched filter: exact Rician ghost false alarms (0.020 at 20 dB, 1.0 at 30 dB for rect vs the twin's 1e-6), ghost zone radius growing `√10` per 10 dB, taper gain exactly sidelobe difference + ENBW loss, and a 60 dB Hamming zone 6× Hann's despite the lower peak sidelobe. Stylised.
- [`research/fog-twin`](fog-twin) — clear-air lidar twin vs fog: detection range `W(αr0)/α` overstated by exactly `e^{αr}`, range error is `W/(1+W)<1` times the extinction error, a policy sized on median fog is violated 50% of the time (5%-quantile range: 5%, 43.5% slower), and one extinction calibrated on a bright target fails for dark ones in patchy fog. Stylised.
- [`research/rain-twin`](rain-twin) — clear-air or mean-rain-rate radar twin vs intermittent storm-cell attenuation: Jensen gap 1.52× in the rain-rate power law, Lambert-W mean-rate range reached by only 39% of real fields, mean-matching barely helps, a rain ensemble scores Brier 0.038 vs 0.101 clear air. Stylised.
- [`research/occlusion-twin`](occlusion-twin) — lidar twin with independent per-beam visibility vs a Poisson occluder field: beams closer than an occluder diameter share occluders, exact blind probability by inclusion–exclusion over union lengths, 8 beams at 25 cm blind 0.0815 vs the twin's 0.0039 (20.9×), the twin is exact once spacing ≥ 2ρ, an occluder-field ensemble matches. Stylised.
- [`research/interference-twin`](interference-twin) — interference-free or mean-noise-rise radar twin vs intermittent mutual radar interference between robots: exact closed-form per-frame Pd, real Pd plateaus at 0.656 (no-hit probability), mean-rise twin 9% optimistic at Pd ≥ 0.9 yet badly pessimistic for hit-tolerant missions, geometry knowledge helps 9-of-10 missions (Brier 0.089 vs 0.101). Stylised.
- [`research/shot-twin`](shot-twin) — constant-variance Gaussian camera twin of Poisson photon counting: the real Bayes threshold is the logarithmic mean, the twin's the arithmetic mean (7.28 vs 8.00 for 4 vs 12), a detector tuned in it has 1.25–1.83× the optimal error and 23× at λ0=200 (λ1=2λ0), and a square-root twin recovers the optimum in 8 of 9 pairs. Stylised.
- [`research/stereo-twin`](stereo-twin) — Gaussian-depth-noise stereo twin vs real disparity-space noise: median exact, gated mean biased by ≈s², over-range errors up to 191× more frequent at 40 m, a twin 3σ margin exceeded 10× as often, mean-depth averaging floors while averaging disparity converges (negative result at the 60 m range floor). Stylised.
- [`research/interleave-twin`](interleave-twin) — independent-loss link twin vs a bursty loss chain for repeated map updates: exact residual `p·q_g^(r−1)`, the twin's 5 back-to-back copies leave 81–311× the claimed loss at λ=0.5–0.8, spacing needs 6–36 slots for two copies, and with ≤8 copies the best design is 12–154 slots of latency vs the twin's 4.
- [`research/watchdog-twin`](watchdog-twin) — i.i.d.-loss heartbeat twin vs a bursty Gilbert link: a 5-missed-beat watchdog false-trips every 3.4e6 ticks in the twin but every 235–990 on the real link, worst at burst length ≈ m (mean time to trip ≈ e·m/p), and a 1e6-tick target needs m = 16–154 instead of the twin's 5. Stylised.
- [`research/deadtime-twin`](deadtime-twin) — linear photon-counter twin of a SPAD lidar vs dead time: half the flux reported at nτ=1, a paralyzable-detector ambiguity (one reading, two fluxes, 12.7× apart at mτ=0.2), first-photon range bias −N/(2√π)σ saturating near −2σ (−22.6 cm at N=10, σ=1 ns) that does not average out, and a Coates correction that removes it. Stylised.
- [`research/gate-twin`](gate-twin) — Gaussian-sensor twin vs a real sensor with outliers under an innovation gate: the twin says the gate costs 8.5% MSE, the real sensor gains 3.0× from it, the worst outlier scale for a gated filter is finite (κ≈4) while ungated error grows as κ², and a gate locks out recovery from a jump for ≈76·D² steps. Stylised.
- [`research/groundeffect-twin`](groundeffect-twin) — rotorcraft altitude twin without ground effect vs real thrust gain `1/(1−(R/4z)²)`: hover sits 18 mm high at one rotor radius (twin: 0), and a delayed loop with 28% twin headroom goes unstable at 0.54 rotor radii. Stylised.
- [`research/chirp-twin`](chirp-twin) — FMCW range–Doppler coupling: a twin that reads true range misses a bias of `f_D T` cells (0.6–4.8 at 30 m/s), signal-level check ≤1e-4 cells, wrapped-Doppler compensation is worse than none on half of the over-range speeds, and a ±60° stationary scene leaves `0.71κv` position bias plus `0.46κv` distortion.
